Home Office cloud services contract may net AWS £1B

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Planning notice puts renewal at nearly twice the 2023 value, just as Whitehall frets about US tech dependence

Amazon Web Services (AWS) looks set to win more than a billion pounds of UK taxpayers' money under a new Home Office contract for public cloud hosting.

A planning notice, published on October 30, covers the renewal of the department's public cloud services, to meet its current and future hosting needs. The estimated value is £881,621,007 ($1.18 billion), which passes £1 billion once VAT is included.

AWS won the existing contract with the Home Office in 2023 for about £450 million (c $603 million). The Register has asked the Home Office why the figure has risen so much, and will update this article if it responds.

Once awarded, the contract is expected to run from December 2027 to December 2029, with a possible extension to December 2031 (four years in total). A market engagement event for suppliers on the G-Cloud 15 Lot 1A procurement framework is scheduled for December 8, giving bidders the chance to pitch.

The Home Office would not say who else is being considered, saying it does not comment on ongoing procurement.

The notice lands as digital sovereignty becomes a bigger problem in the UK and across Europe, amid suspicion that US authorities have cut off access to American technology for political reasons. A survey found that about three-quarters of businesses in the UK, France, and Germany fear Washington could abruptly sever access to the US tech platforms they rely on.

Earlier this year, the Open Rights Group warned politicians that the UK is far too dependent on US technology companies to run critical operations.

Whitehall is mindful of its over-reliance on American hyperscalers. As The Register revealed in 2024, the Cabinet Office admitted - in an internal document seen by us - that the government’s negotiating power had been inhibited by vendor lock-in.

It said the "UK government’s current approach to cloud adoption and management across its departments faces several challenges" which combined result "in risk concentration and vendor lock-in that inhibit UK government’s negotiating power over the cloud vendors." It acknowledged "the existing dominance of AWS and Azure in the UK Government’s cloud services is set to continue."

A source at one supplier that works with the public sector said the UK administration was "failing to support UK [cloud] providers, it says they can’t meet scale and that is garbage." They claimed that in many cases the government people writing the tenders are advised by professional services companies to favor the hyperscalers. The professional services companies are then "rewarded" as migration or integration partners, our source claimed.

The UK government also pledged to steer more business to  small-to-medium sized enterprises (SMEs) in an action plan published last month. In August it added smaller, local suppliers to G-Cloud 15, though a place on the framework does not guarantee any business.

Pledges like this are nothing new, and they have largely come to nothing, as we reported in 2005, 2013, 2016, and 2018.

According to public sector intelligence platform BidSkim, AWS takes 75 percent of the government money on cloud contracts, worth about £1.22 billion ($1.6 billion). It estimates that Microsoft Azure has more contracts, but these are smaller and add up to £355 million ($475 million), while Google Cloud is on £47 million ($63 million) and Oracle Cloud $15 million ($20 million).

'Not doing so well' - Alan Shearer's Leeds United admission as prediction for Tottenham Hotspur clash shared

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Premier League legend Alan Shearer has acknowledged Leeds United’s form has tailed off - but still believes they will wrestle a point from Tottenham Hotspur tomorrow (November 7).

Leeds are winless in five Premier League games, having lost two and drawn three since their 4-1 rout of Newcastle United on September 14.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Spurs, meanwhile, are turning a corner after a dismal start to the 2026/27 campaign.

Shearer told Betfair: “This should be a good game. Leeds are not doing so well at the minute. Spurs have found a bit of form, but Elland Road's a tough place to go. I'm going to go for a draw.

“I think he's got something going [Roberto] De Zerbi, he's got a little bit of belief there and you just need something to happen like the red cards for the opposition, but I wouldn't look too much into that. I think Spurs will be all right. I still think they need a forward, but I think it'll be a draw there, point apiece.

“De Zerbi has turned them around, but he needed to, and they've had to be patient, but he stayed calm. I think that's the most important thing.”

Leeds could be without Anton Stach and Noah Okafor, who have both seeing their preparations for the game interrupted by injury.

Dominic Calvert-Lewin also missed a session with a foot issue, but there is hope he could be fit enough to lead the line against Spurs.

Whites boss Daniel Farke said: “It's not the easiest situation at the moment especially offensive-wise, but we want to stay together and fight together and also make sure that in perhaps not the best situation, we still are able to dig in and grind out a result in this important game.”

Serhou Guirassy to miss Borussia Dortmund’s next game due to injury: The diagnosis

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Serhou Guirassy will miss Borussia Dortmund’s next game due to injury, Niko Kovac has confirmed during his press conference.

Borussia Dortmund are due to play Bundesliga newcomers and this season’s surprise package, SV Elversberg, on Saturday afternoon. However, BVB will now be without their main striker following an injury sustained during their midweek victory in the Champions League against Real Betis.

“Serhou has a strain and will therefore not be able to participate tomorrow”, Kovac explained.

It’s unclear whether the 30-year-old forward will join up with the Guinean national team ahead of the international break once the weekend concludes. For Kovac, he believes that the striker will remain in Germany for treatment:

“We’ll see how we proceed in the coming days; we’re in close contact with the federation. He will likely stay here, but we’ll discuss that further.”

With the absence of Guirassy, there will be opportunities for Maximilian Beier and Fabio Silva to lead the line up front.

De Bruyne, Moreira and Keita among Serie A stars in Belgium squad to face Italy

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Five Serie A players have been included in the latest Belgium national team squad by Mark van Bommel, including Napoli star Kevin De Bruyne and Milan’s derby hero Diego Moreira.

Belgium will be in Nations League action in November, taking on Turkiye away from home on Thursday, November 12, before hosting Italy in Brussels on Sunday, November 15.

As things stand, France top the group with 10 points from their first four matches of the Nations League group stage, Italy sit in second on seven points, Belgium are third on six points and Turkiye are in fourth place with zero points.

The Belgium squad includes four players who play their club football in Italy, including Napoli’s De Bruyne. He is coming off a difficult period with the Partenopei, who have lost seven of their first 14 competitive matches of the 2026-27 season, including two over the last week.

It has been a slightly more encouraging week for Moreira, who scored and provided two assists in Milan’s 3-1 derby della Madonnina victory over rivals and reigning Serie A champions Inter last weekend. Milan have since fallen to a disappointing 2-0 defeat at home against Ferencvaros in the Europa League, but the Belgian was not introduced until half-time of that game.

There are also call-ups for Atalanta’s Charles De Ketelaere, who is hoping to reach double figures for goals for Belgium this international break, Parma midfielder Mandela Keita and Bologna defender Arthur Theate.

Goalkeepers: Senne Lammens (Manchester United), Maarten Vandevoort (RB Leipzig), Mike Penders (Chelsea), Jari De Busser (AZ Alkmaar).

Defenders: Timothy Castagne (Fulham), Kyriani Sabbe (Club Brugge), Nathan Ngoy (Lille), Brandon Mechele (Club Brugge), Arthur Theate (Bologna), Josue Kongolo (Genk), Maxim De Cuyper (Brighton), Joaquin Seys (Club Brugge).

Midfielders: Romeo Lavia (Chelsea), Nicolas Raskin (Rangers), Your Tielemans (Manchester United), Mandela Keita (Parma), Kevin De Bruyne (Napoli), Hans Vanaken (Club Brugge).

Forwards: Diego Moreira (Milan), Jeremy Doku (Manchester City), Dodi Lukebakio (Benfica), Mika Godts (PSG), Leandro Trossard (Besiktas), Romelu Lukaku (Fenerbahce), Hugo Cuypers (Monterrey).

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Thomas Tuchel hands Jobe Bellingham the chance to carve his own path as an England star

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

From childhood Champions League finals on a patch of grass in Stourbridge to the biggest stage in international football, Jobe Bellingham is determined to step out of his brother’s shadow

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Jobe Bellingham’s football career is strikingly similar to that of his big brother Jude, from the unusual Birmingham-to-Dortmund route right down to their distinctive rangey stride, and so far it has been a case of, anything you can do, I did it faster, better, with a little more panache. But there is one thing Jude didn’t tick off on his meteoric rise – he never captained England Under-21s.

Proudly wearing the armband in September, Jobe shone in a 4-2 comeback win over Kazakhstan in qualifying for next year’s U21 Euros. It was another sign that the 21-year-old’s development is rapidly accelerating.

“He was outstanding,” U21s manager Lee Carsley said. “I went to watch Jobe a couple of weeks ago in Dortmund and he’s progressing so quickly. Physically he’s grown. He’s definitely got that strength in his legs. He’s moving the ball so well and his footwork has really improved. The way he’s moving the ball, taking opponents on, and using his strength and balance … he was always a good player but now he’s going through the levels.”

That, Thomas Tuchel stressed in his Wembley press conference on Friday, was the primary reason for Jobe’s first call-up to the senior England squad, a landmark earned “on his own merit”. The Bellingham brothers are both included in the 25-man group to face Croatia here on Thursday and Spain in Madrid on Sunday, as England – who have virtually qualified for the Nations League quarter-finals in March – go all out to win Group A3.

“Lee is speaking very, very highly of him,” Tuchel explained. “In the Under-21s, he’s a standout player since a long time now. He’s the captain. So I think the door should always be open for that pathway, for special performances over a long period of time, and that’s what Jobe has done.

“He has also found his rhythm with Dortmund, he’s had an excellent start to the season. He’s a regular starter now there and very influential. So there’s an opportunity coming on his own merit. Jobe deserves to make an impression and that’s why we’re glad to have him in camp and see what he can do for us.”

Jobe wears his first name on the back of his shirt, partly in an effort to distinguish himself from his brother, now a fully fledged galactico at Real Madrid who shone at the 2026 World Cup and already has 60 international caps to his name at 23.

“When I signed for Sunderland it was suggested to me by someone, a special coach I had there,” Jobe explained. “I was still young and thought it was cool to have my own name on the back. It didn’t have any deeper meaning but it’s become something unique. It shows that I’m going my own way. Some people don’t like it – I don’t care.”

The comparisons, of course, are inevitable. Both brothers made their debuts for Birmingham City aged 16 before moving to Dortmund – Jude aged 17 in a £25m transfer, Jobe via a successful two-season stint at Sunderland where he won Championship Young Player of the Season, later moving to the German club aged 19 for £28m.

Anyone would think Jobe might choose a different club in order to carve his own path, but he recognised that it would be damaging to make career choices simply to avoid his brother’s footprints, even if he had “fears” before joining Dortmund, where Jude grew into a global star. “It’s not about being different,” Jobe said. “It’s about doing the right thing.”

The brothers are close. They spent every possible minute of their childhood playing football on a small square of grass outside the family home in Stourbridge, up until it got dark. “Every game we played, we always said it was the Champions League final,” said Jude in 2022, two years before winning the actual Champions League final with Real Madrid. “We’d make up teams and make up stadiums that we’d play in. We must have played about 1,000 Champions League finals on that little patch of grass.”

They are both fast, strong and physically imposing central midfielders, standing 6ft plenty, with huge technical ability, coached into them by their father Mark who had a nomadic career as a prolific striker in non-league football. Incidentally, Mark Bellingham has been starring for England recently too, after scoring seven goals on his debut for the England Over-50s Veterans in a 13-1 win against the Republic of Ireland.

But there are also differences in how they play the game. Jude’s strengths are in an advanced No 8 or No 10 role, surging forwards with the ball at his feet, gliding past tacklers, holding off markers and impacting the game with goals. Jobe is more of a traditional box-to-box midfielder with an excellent reading of the game, someone who sits a little deeper and can lay a platform for more attacking teammates.

There is another difference. Jobe is an altogether more low-key figure, and has said he does not wish to “be a superstar... I don’t want to be a big character away from the pitch. When I play I just want to be one of the highest performing players and get my head down.”

Jobe now has the opportunity to begin his own journey as an England international. He will do so not as Jude’s younger brother, but as a midfielder who has earned his chance. Though if he does make his senior debut, Jobe might just happen to have his biggest fan beside him. “As long as he’s happy, that’s all I really care about,” Jude said recently. “His happiness means more to me than my own.”

Goalkeepers: Jordan Pickford, Jason Steele, James Trafford.

Defenders: Jarrad Branthwaite, Marc Guehi, Lewis Hall, Ezri Konsa, Jarell Quansah, Djed Spence, Trent Alexander-Arnold, Ben White.

Midfielders: Elliot Anderson, Jobe Bellingham, Jude Bellingham, James Garner, Josh King, Myles Lewis-Skelly, Declan Rice.

Forwards: Harry Kane, Dominic Calvert-Lewin, Eberechi Eze, Morgan Gibbs-White, Anthony Gordon, Rio Ngumoha, Morgan Rogers

Crystanbul revisited: The night Liverpool’s title hopes spectacularly fell apart

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

When you think back to Liverpool’s stunning collapse to surrender the 2013-14 Premier League title, chances are you remember the Steven Gerrard slip.

That came with three games to go. Chelsea were the visitors and Gerrard’s untimely loss of footing allowed Demba Ba to run through and score.

The west London side would go on to win the game 2-0, with Jose Mourinho banging the Chelsea badge on his blue gilet in front of the away fans.

To mark the Year of the Horse, we tell Chelsea-related tales from previous such cycles in the Lunar New Year… 👀🐎 https://t.co/cxtAKVaSqV

— Chelsea FC (@ChelseaFC) February 17, 2026

But if the loss to Chelsea was the dagger in the heart of Liverpool’s title bid, the next game was the decapitation.

At a time when cooler heads were struggling to prevail, it was hard to see Liverpool’s loss to Chelsea as anything but gift-wrapping the Premier League trophy to Manchester City.

In reality, Liverpool still had a decent chance of winning their first league title in 24 years.

After the Chelsea game, Liverpool had dropped from top spot but only on goal difference with City now leading. The two teams had kept a similar scoring pacing, both nearing 100, but City’s defence had been stronger, giving them a 12-goal difference over their Merseyside opponents.

The path to victory for Liverpool then was to win both games and hope City dropped points or to win by such a margin that they swing the goal difference in their favour.

It wasn’t an unrealistic prospect either, City did have the harder fixtures. While they had two home games, they faced two teams in Aston Villa and West Ham that were yet to be mathematically clear of relegation.

Liverpool meanwhile knew that if they got through the game against Palace, they would play the final match of the season at Anfield against a Newcastle side that was safe in ninth but had a minus 18 goal difference, the worst in the top half of the table.

If given the choice, Brendan Rodgers may well have chosen Palace as the team he fancied to face at this point of the season. They were 11th and already confirmed their place in the Premier League next season. Selhurst Park is about 40 miles away from the coast but Palace should already be on the beach.

Palace were on a good run of form, winning their previous five out of six, but surely, surely Liverpool would comfortably knock them over.

If there was ever a man born to ruin a party, it was Pulis. After the Chelsea defeat, Rodgers complained about the defensive tactics deployed by Mourinho. Pulis probably sat in his wingback armchair at home and a smile appeared across his lips.

The fixture had been selected for the Monday night, meaning Steven Gerrard led his Liverpool team out into the setting South London sun with a point to prove.

The initial focus was on Gerrard. How would the man who was as Liverpool as the Beatles and turning K into Ch when speaking recover from being the man who may have cost them the Premier League title?

That answer was given very quickly. In front of a Crystal Palace stand full of fans that were very clearly up for it, Gerrard whipped in a corner to an unmarked Joe Allen at the back post. The smallest man on the pitch scored against a defence that had not conceded a set-piece goal all season.

The first half ran out as exactly as Liverpool would have hoped. They had 68% of the ball, 12 shots to Palace’s three and were in complete control, much to Pulis’ annoyance.

He told the BBC later that year that he gave his centre-halves Scott Dann and Damien Delaney a b*llocking for not getting close enough to Luis Suarez and Daniel Sturridge, which is easier said than done.

The words of wisdom appeared to go in one ear and out the other as Liverpool scored two quick goals to begin the second half.

Sterling skipped away from the pressure of Joel Ward while both Dann and Delaney had dropped deep to cover the run of Suarez.

The two got closer to tackling each other as Sturridge’s effort deflected off Delaney’s trailing foot and past a helpless Julian Speroni.

Remembering his manager’s half-time words, Delaney soon charges out to try and close down Suarez just past the centre circle in Palace’s half.

His horror show of a second half continues as he instead controls it for Suarez before being completely wrong-footed as the Uruguayan charges at the two Palace defenders now left.

Raheem Sterling was on hand to relieve the pass and pull Dann away from Suarez and even with the English winger’s pass being completely underhit, Dann misses it and Suarez gets it through Speroni’s legs on the way to the net.

Liverpool smelt blood, but in many ways the quick nature of their two goals proved to be their demise.

Rodgers revealed the message at half-time was just to win and that “if it’s 1-0, it’s 1-0.” but asking for calm heads at this time was too much for a team that had been feeding off euphoria.

Ahead of the Chelsea game, Liverpool had won their last 10 Premier League games. They had led the league for the past four weeks and 24 years of pent-up frustration had morphed into a feeling of this finally being the year, of it being destiny. For a long while, Rodgers and the players harnessed that feeling.

This was not just any team either, for you would be hard-pressed to find another city in England where football is more woven into the fabric than it is in Liverpool. They had suffered at the hands of Manchester United and now they needed it to be their turn.

That pandemonium was going through the players’ minds when Suarez raced to grab the ball at 3-0.

It was a powder keg ready to explode and the Selhurst Park faithful needed little reason to believe. The spark that set the whole thing ablaze came from the man who had helped gift Liverpool two goals, Damien Delaney.

In pushing forward for more goals, Liverpool had left themselves open at the back. The midfield pair of Gerrard and Lucas were particularly at fault for allowing Delaney to reach just outside the box completely unmarked.

The back four had all dropped deep, prompting Glen Johnson to neither go with his fellow defenders or close down Delaney. Instead, he stood stiff, waited for Delaney to shoot and turned his body sideways in order to protect himself.

Having been the victim of a deflection earlier in the half, Delaney was now the beneficiary of one. The ball came off the stomach of Johnson and went straight into the top left corner.

At the time, Martin Tyler, who was on commentary for Sky, said it would “get the goal difference back for Liverpool” with the possibility of something even worse not yet entering his mind.

Right then, Rodgers should have reacted. He had two experienced centre-backs in Daniel Agger and Kolo Toure sitting on the bench but they remained uncalled for as Liverpool pushed forward once again.

Just a minute later, Liverpool had seven men in the opposition box when Palace’s Yannick Bolasie skipped past the half-hearted tackle from Philippe Coutinho. Johnson again adopted the ‘stand still and hope I get him’ approach, which Bolasie said ‘thank you very much to’ as he knocked it by him.

The Liverpool players did get back but to nowhere useful. Dwight Gayle gave Jon Flanagan a shoulder feint, meaning he had plenty of room to poke home Bolasie’s pass.

You could barely hear the stadium announcer announcing the goal over the sound of the crowd. Palace was up for it. Selhurst Park was up for it. Liverpool were collapsing under the fear of their own nightmare.

This time Rodgers did react, but he only took off Sturridge for Victor Moses who at this point in his career was still a winger.

The midfield two of Lucas and Gerrard were completely bypassed once again as a chipped ball was controlled by the chest of Glenn Murray.

Martin Skrtel had come out to try and get close to Murray, leaving a gaping hole for Gayle. He waited for the ball to bounce before slotting it past Mignolet. The Liverpool collapse was complete.

“Liverpool have caved in,” shouted Tyler. Rodgers had a blank expression on his face, masking what must have been absolute despair on the inside.

The final whistle blew soon after. Liverpool, whose modern history had been built on the overturning of a 3-0 deficit, had just been on the wrong side of that very moment.

Gerrard was on his haunches. Suarez cried into his shirt as he bent over. With that, Liverpool’s title hopes were over.

“There is no doubt Manchester City will go on and win it,” said Rodgers. “We needed to win to keep the pressure on going into the last game and we did not do that.”

Winning the Premier League title would have been the apex of Rodgers’ career. He would have had a figure as beloved as Klopp. Maybe they could have kept hold of Suarez for another year and made a push in the Champions League.

That night in South London completely changed the trajectory of Liverpool Football Club. The always next year team would have to wait another year at least. In the end, they had to wait another six.

Since that night, Liverpool have won 10 times at Selhurst Park, including a 7-0 thumping, and yet Selhurst Park will always be a painful memory.

Nine minutes of madness had defined a whole season in one of the greatest Premier League moments of all time.

Review: KLM 787-10 Business Class (Amsterdam – Los Angeles), Still A Solid Choice

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

I don’t need an airline to surprise me every time. Sometimes I want to know what I’m getting. My KLM 787-10 business class flight from Amsterdam to Los Angeles reminded me why I keep choosing KLM…and why I still wish it would turn down the heat!

I recently flew KLM’s Boeing 787-10 in World Business Class from Amsterdam to Los Angeles once again, about a year after the flight reviewed below. The seat and overall experience have changed little, so I’ll offer a more abbreviated review of my latest flight. My comprehensive review follows, with a detailed look at the cabin and seat features. In short: nothing has changed at KLM, for better or for worse.

Yes, especially for 60,000 Flying Blue miles (though I’d probably now pay the extra to get out of “light” class). The seat was comfortable and the food was excellent. I enjoyed the flight even though I knew almost exactly what to expect.

The warm cabin remains my biggest complaint, particularly because I encountered the same issue on this route last year. Individual air vents would help accommodate those of us who prefer a cooler sleeping environment.

Even so, this was a comforting way to get home to Los Angeles. Familiar and even boring does not have to mean disappointing.

The detailed review below covers my April 2025 flight on the same aircraft type and route. It includes more detailed info on the seat features, inflight entertainment, Wi-Fi, lavatories, and amenity kit.

My 11-hour KLM flight from Amsterdam to Los Angeles on the 787-10 in World Business Class was smooth, comfortable, but a little warm…

I’ve reviewed the KLM A330, 777, and 787-9 in business class and also the 787-10 in economy class, but this was my first time flying the 787-10 in business class.

I arrived at the KLM Crown Lounge quite early to get my work done for the day, just in case the internet did not function on my flight. While the lounge gets crowded, I quite like it.

Boarding began at 9:05, 45 minutes before departure.

I entered via door 2L and proceeded through the galley and turned left to my seat in 4K.

The KLM 787-10 business class cabin features 38 Jamco America Venture seats arranged in a 1-2-1 reverse herringbone configuration. These Japanese-made seats are 10-15% lighter than traditional reverse herringbone seats.

Seat pitch is generous and seats convert into a fully lie-flat bed (75 inches long). There’s a side storage compartment, seat controls on the armrest, two USB-A ports (one on the side and one under the screen), and a universal power outlet.

My left armrest could be raised (for use when seated) and lowered (to give more space for sleeping).

Interestingly, I noticed the seat looked a bit tattered…at least cosmetically. The paint was chipped on the armrest and under the monitor and the (faux) leather shell around the seat was discolored. There was also noticeable dirt and grime. Everything functioned, but it really looked like an old product, even though my aircraft, PH-BKG, was only delivered five years ago.

Bedding on KLM is comparatively average, with a large pillow and soft duvet-style blanket. There is no day blanket or mattress pad.

I tried to sleep after lunch but the cabin was warm and there were no personal air vents. I did manage a few hours of sleep, but I would have loved to sleep eight hours…a cool cabin and a plush mattress pad would have helped.

The retrofitted 777 World Business Class cabins offer suites with closing doors; the 787-10 do not have this extra feature.

The 11-hour journey to California included lunch after takeoff, a midflight snack, and a meal before landing. Menus were waiting on each seat and KLM continues to print menus for each flight (you’ll notice on the backside of the menu lists your flight number and flight date).

Pre-departure beverages were offered before takeoff, including beer, sparkling wine, orange juice, and “Royal Blue Legend,” the KLM signature cocktail with lemon, ginger, and spirulina…which I had to try.

Lunch began after takeoff, with a refreshing amuse bouche (I don’t even know what it was, but the green thing was topped in a toasted walnut and was very nice) and a drink (I enjoyed “The Very High Fashioned” cocktail, a riff on an Old Fashioned).

I had chili shrimps with pickled watermelon and beetroot mousse and fresh watercress for my appetizer…a great collection of flavors. I was served with a small green salad and bread with Dutch butter. I switched to a Boschendal Boschen Blane 2023 (white wine from South Africa).

For my main course, I chose salmon à la Flamande with Hollandaise sauce, served with white asparagus, scrambled eggs, and potatoes. The salmon filet was very small, but it was a good choice.

For dessert, cheese (blue cheese and Beemster Royaal Fenugreek cheese) with crackers, grapes, and a raisin salad with pine nuts, followed by a tart raspberry mousse topped with sunflower seeds, topped with passion fruit crumble.

About halfway through the flight, passengers were offered a vegetarian croquette sandwich with mustard (I tried that) or a mini club sandwich. Both were served with cherry cheesecake ice cream, which I skipped.

A self-serve station was also set up in the galley with Dutch snacks:

105 minutes before landing, a Caesar salad with pulled chicken, egg, tomatoes, Reypenaar cheese, and pine nuts was offered along with a side of pumpkin ravioli in cream artichoke sauce. A very nice pre-arrival meal, though the pasta was served wrapped in aluminum foil…that could have easily been removed.

The internet worked well on the flight (the flight pass was €22) and it turned out to be a very productive flight when it became clear I could not sleep.

I also managed one movie on the flight, an old Brad Pitt, Kevin Spacey, and Morgan Freeman film called Seven. A little jarring, to say the least…

The KLM IFE library includes music, movies, TV shows, games, and a moving flight map.

KLM’s 787-10 has two lavatories in the front of the cabin reserved for business class passengers (and also for crew use). As always on KLM, I appreciate the tulip flower and the Delft Blue House wallpaper. Soap products were from Marie-Stella-Maris.

The amenity kit also had products from Marie-Stella-Maris (face cream and lip balm) and also included socks, a pen, earplugs, and a dental kit (with tootpaste tablets instead of a tube of toothpaste).

As we approached LAX, the purser came around with Delft Blue Houses, the signature “souvenir” of KLM World Business Class. I was hoping for house 98, which was the parental home of Anthony Fokker in Haarlem (Fokker went on to become one of the world’s greatest aircraft builder in the late 1920’s with factories in the Netherlands and the USA) and I got it!

Then the lady in front of me did not want a house, so I asked if I could have hers and got a second one, 89.

The crew was very seasoned/mature (as always on KLM, it seems) and also very lovely. Very attentive service with a smile and I appreciated so much that flight attendants roamed the cabin throughout the flight, not just during the formal meal services.

It was a beautiful morning (yes, still morning) when we landed at LAX 11 hours later and we flew right over downtown LA and the iconic In-N-Out next to LAX.

KLM offers a very average business class, which is to say a very solid business class. I enjoyed good service, great food, and a comfortable seat on the flight, though the lack of air vent and warm cabin made sleeping difficult. I like the 787-10 and look forward to flying KLM again.

Netflix’s Animated Twilight Adaptation Confirmed for Two Seasons

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Love it or hate it, you've probably encountered the Twilight series at some point in your life. And the popularity of the vampire romantic drama is not over yet: Netflix is taking Midnight Sun, a 2020 novel that retells Twilight from Edward Cullen's perspective, and turning it into an animated series. It is now confirmed that the Midnight Sun adaptation will be a two-season series.

Twilight is a fantasy movie starring Robert Pattinson and Kristen Stewart, and it took the world by storm in 2008. It is an adaptation of Stephenie Meyer's 2005 vampire romance book. Despite being heavily parodied at the time, it had a following of millions. For many, it was the best thing since sliced bread. Whether you fall into that category or not, you might be interested in Netflix's animated series adaptation.

Netflix is dipping its toes into the Twilight saga with the release of an animated show called Midnight Sun, based on the 2020 novel by Stephenie Meyer. While the original Twilight book is narrated by Bella Swan, Midnight Sun goes through the same events but from Edward Cullen's point of view.

Rather than seeing Bella figure out who and what Edward is, Midnight Sun lets readers understand what goes through his mind as a vampire navigating the world and as he falls in love with Bella. It's an interesting perspective, and we get first-hand experience of what he was thinking at key points in the story — like the biology classroom scene — which is completely different compared to Bella's point of view.

Despite Midnight Sun being only one book, Netflix is extending the adaptation into a two-season series. Actors were being actively cast for the series this summer, and reports indicate that production was scheduled to take place between August 2026 and May 2027. Beyond that, not much has been revealed, which isn't surprising since Twilight fans will know most of the plot details already.

Midnight Sun might sound like a strange pick for an adaptation, but the popularity of the franchise hasn't waned yet. Whether it's because of nostalgia or genuine love for the story and characters, the Twilight series is still able to rise to the top of the charts nearly 20 years later.

Fans are eager to return to the gloomy town of Forks, Washington, but the series is not coming out just yet. Netflix's Midnight Sun is currently in development, and it does not have a release window at the time of writing.

Steam gets a boatload of restored Wing Commanders and Ultima ports after EA producer stumbles across cupboard of old tapes

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"He took me over there, opened it up and it was like Indiana Jones"

EA have stuck a bunch of restored versions and ports of classic games, on Steam, GOG and the EA App. 25 games in all, including multiple entries in the Wing Commander and Ultima series, are part of the haul. Why's this happening now? Well, an EA producer stumbled across a cupboard full of old tapes, film reels and CDs.

According to an interview with The Game Business, the discovery of this old gubbins was a consequence of EA senior producer Jim Vessella needing somewhere new to stick a bunch of Betacam tapes used to help make the Command & Conquer Remastered Collection. "One of the facilities people was like, 'I think there’s a room in the other building that has a couple of items like that'," Vessella recalled. "He took me over there, opened it up and it was like Indiana Jones… just, whoa. Knowing what I’d discovered with the Command & Conquer tapes, I really felt like we had to find out what was on all these tapes."

The producer was able to convince EA's bosses that this stuff was worth the work needed to stick it back on storefronts, and lo, an EA blog post gushing about their vault's door having slid aside to reveal this pile of retro games. The collection includes restored versions of the following games, all with "robust quality-of-life updates and higher resolution Full-Motion Videos (FMV) from the Master tapes":

Meanwhile, the rest of the lineup is filled out by "faithful ports" of the following:

On Steam, all of these are currently on sale for less than a fiver each, with the prices of the most expensive ones set to rise to just under £10 when the introductory offer expires on November 19th.

It's good news in preservation terms, though I'm not sure it totally washes away the bad vibes of EA's Saudi and private equity owners reportedly looking to take over Unity.

Steyning Town edged out by AFC Croydon Athletic in thriller

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The sides were separated by just two points in the table and the Barrowmen headed into the fixture full of confidence following an impressive 2-0 victory over Sittingbourne in their previous outing.

The opening ten minutes proved to be a fairly even contest, with neither side able to establish control.

Did you know with an ad-lite subscription to SussexWorld, you get 70% fewer ads while viewing the news that matters to you.

The first meaningful opportunity did not arrive until the 18th minute, when James Taylor tried his luck from outside the box. However, his effort was comfortably gathered by Steyning goalkeeper Alfie Mansell.

Steyning continued to struggle to create any real attacking threat. Croydon began to find more opportunities, with Jayden Raymond testing Mansell in the 24th minute. The Steyning keeper pushed the ball behind for a corner.

That brought further danger for the hosts, but Harvey Woolard produced an important goal-line clearance. Steyning looked to respond four minutes later from a corner, but Croydon keeper Amaadou Tangarn was on hand to punch the delivery clear.

Croydon continued to threaten, with Luke Dreher trying his luck in the 34th minute. Once again, Mansell was equal to the effort.

The hosts finally managed to register a shot on target in the 38th minute as Ryan Worrall struck a low effort towards goal, but his attempt drifted wide.

And Steyning’s resistance was broken just two minutes later. Raymond found himself unmarked and made no mistake, driving a low effort into the bottom corner to give Croydon the lead.

Things quickly went from bad to worse for Steyning as Raymond struck again in the 42nd minute. The forward was perfectly positioned to turn home a close-range tap-in, doubling Croydon’s advantage and leaving the hosts with an uphill battle.

Steyning attempted to find a response before the break, with Jesse Sentamu sending an effort over the crossbar in the 45th minute. Another opportunity followed from a corner but the resulting header also sailed over.

Steyning returned for the second half looking to turn the game around, but once again made a slow start, struggling to find a way through Croydon’s organised defensive line.

The hosts eventually found a breakthrough in the 61st minute. A deflected cross earned Steyning a corner, and following a scramble inside the area, substitute Max Blencowe managed to find the back of the net to make it 2-1.

Croydon continued to hold their defensive shape and limit Steyning’s opportunities. A free-kick for the hosts in the 66th minute was comfortably caught by goalkeeper Amaadou Tangarn, before James Taylor delivered a dangerous cross a minute later.

The Croydon defence could only spoon the ball behind for another corner, but Steyning were unable to make anything of the resulting set-piece.

However, the momentum was beginning to shift. Just two minutes later, substitute Alex Brewer produced a moment of real quality, striking a beautiful effort from outside the box to level the scores at 2-2 and complete an impressive comeback.

Mansell was called into action twice in quick succession, producing two important saves to keep Steyning level and prevent Croydon from immediately restoring their advantage.

But the visitors were not prepared to settle for a point. In the 77th minute, Josh Williams produced a brilliant finish to put Croydon back in front at 3-2.

Steyning pushed hard for another equaliser in the closing stages, but Croydon remained disciplined at the back, quickly closing down space and preventing the hosts from delivering the ball into dangerous areas.

A late opportunity arrived in the 88th minute, with Blencowe looking to grab his second, but his effort was comfortably dealt with by Tangarn. Brewer then came close to finding another late goal in the 90th minute, only to send his effort over the crossbar.

Croydon’s Noah Watson received a yellow card in the 94th minute for time-wasting as Steyning struggled to maintain possession during the closing moments. The hosts earned one final corner in the 95th minute after another effort went wide, but the set-piece came to nothing.

The final whistle confirmed a frustrating 3-2 defeat for Steyning, who had shown tremendous character to fight back from two goals down and restore parity, only for Williams’ late intervention to deny them a share of the points.

Steyning are 15th and host Horley Town on Saturday.

Protesters Break Into Microsoft Data Center Site And Hang ‘FCK AI’ Banner

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

As the global markets dive in reaction to the Democratic sweep in the U.S. midterm elections, fearful of a party willing to take a stand on the catastrophic spread of AI data centers, the question is being asked whether the public’s hatred of these environment-demolishing monstrosities could have played a part in how people voted. At the same time, protests against the development of town-sized data centers are springing up all around the world. In the latest example, as reported by ReutersÂ (via The Verge), Microsoft’s construction site in Amsterdam was broken into this morning by around 100 people, some of whom climbed a crane and hung a banner reading “FCK AI.”

Others, according toÂ The Verge, revealed a banner reading, “Microsoft powers genocide,” referencing the corporate behemoth’s ongoing support of the Netanyahu regime in Israel, along with “Free Palestine” and “Microsoft has got to go.”

Footage of the protesters has been uploaded to YouTube, showing people scaling the gates and piling into Microsoft’s construction site, with some scaling a crane to strap a makeshift banner reading “FCK AI” down its main arm, while others march and chant, the messaging seemingly split between those campaigning against data centers and those objecting to the Israeli attacks on Palestine.

Amsterdam has already taken a harder line than many other areas regarding the building of data centers, announcing last year that it would no longer approve projects submitted after the end of 2023, nor the expansion of previously approved sites, citing that the city lacks the space and the power to support them. The city said it wouldn’t start considering new pitches until 2030.

Meanwhile, polling suggests that the widespread construction of data centers in the U.S. was a factor in how people voted during the midterms. 56 percent of those surveyed by AP reported that the topic was a “crucial or major” factor in who they voted for, and 76 percent were against data centers being built within their own community.

20 Steals From Backcountry’s Early Black Friday Sale

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

I love Black Friday. I also hate Black Friday. Sure, you can snag unbelievable bargains on outdoor gear on what’s become the national holiday of capitalism, but it’s also easy to get lost in the digital vortex. There are just so many sales to choose from, and often those sales are limited to a brief window. Decision paralysis and exhaustion ensue—even if you haven’t loaded up on tryptophan-laced turkey.

That said, I’m happy to report that Backcountry’s Early Black Friday sale starts today, November 6th. While the online outfitter will add heaps of gear to the sale as Black Friday approaches, the early sale gives bargain hunters weeks to shop.

Even better? Right now, Backcountry is offering up to 20% off full-price items and an extra 10 to 20% off clearance, from high-end brands like The North Face, Blundstone, Dakine, Black Diamond, and more. No code necessary, just add your wish list gear and the discount shows up in your cart when you’re signed in to your (free) Summit Club Membership. And if you’re a Summit+ Member, you get even more savings, like an extra $50 cash back on certain deals.

This is pretty cool. I write about the best gear sales every month for Outside, and this discount code gives shoppers a rare opportunity to scoop up current gear (not last season’s inventory) at a double-digit discount. To celebrate, I rounded up 20 picks from Backcountry’s Early Black Friday Sale, all of which I’ve personally tested or am currently putting on the chopping block here in Tahoe.

Long story short: keep reading for my top picks or check out the sale in its entirety here.

Three decades after its debut, The North Face dug into the archives and dropped an alpine-ready version of the iconic Nuptse Jacket (available in both men’s and women’s styles). Generously stuffed with 800-fill goose down (Backcountry’s current product description says 700-fill, but The North Face claims higher-grade 800-fill down here), the Summit Nuptse puffy is as warm as they come. I love the boxy cut and classic color-blocked colorways—style elements that are at home in mountain towns and metros alike. In my opinion, this puffy is overkill on the slopes, but I wouldn’t hesitate to recommend it as a winter camping basecamp parka or a belay puffy. That said, you can also deploy it as a daily winter essential, whether you’re partaking in parking lot après at your local ski resort or on a frigid dog walk.

This pairing of the Smith Method Pro MIPS helmet and the Squad MAG goggle has everything I’m looking for in a helmet-and-goggle combo. The helmet offers solid protection thanks to a MIPS liner and in-mold construction, while the grid-style ear pads are comfortable and removable to boot. I love the low-profile silhouette of the helmet, the built-in ventilation, and the quick-and-easy magnetic buckle. It’s also light enough for me to bring on backcountry tours. The Method Pro’s flat brim fits seamlessly with the Squad MAG, a stylish cylindrical goggle that features Smith’s streamlined lens interchangeability system and top-of-the-line ChromaPop contrast-enhancing lens tech. Separately, I’m a big fan of these Smith options, but together, they’re a dynamic duo that can seriously amp up your on-hill enjoyment.

I’ve never had a pair of Smartwool base layers that I haven’t liked—and that’s saying something, as I’ve tested enough of the brand’s thermal merino offerings over the last 15 years to have a fresh set of long johns for every day of the week. One of the alluring aspects of merino wool, however, is the natural fiber’s antimicrobial and anti-odor properties, so if you’re traveling, camping, or only have one set, you can wear these Smartwool pieces multiple days in a row without inciting the ire of your ski buddies. These base layers are crafted from 100% merino wool, and the mid-heavyweight fabric is toasty and ready for sub-zero days on the slopes.

I just got my hands on this Norrona Lofoten Gore-Tex Pro kit last week, and I took it out during the first snowfall of the season here in Tahoe. It withstood fat, sopping flakes with no problem—unsurprising considering that it’s crafted with Gore-Tex Pro, what many (myself included) consider to be the best-in-class membrane for skiing and snowboarding. I was surprised, however, by how much I dug the style (the two-tone red is unabashedly fly), and I love how the pant and jacket zip together for extra defense on the deepest of days. I can’t offer anything beyond these first impressions right now, but if you’re in the market for premium outerwear—and want to save some money while you’re at it—this Scandinavian alpine armor merits a closer look.

While I appreciate the breathability of a softshell kit for touring, I love to chase powder and shred through storm cycles. I also call the moisture-prone Sierra home. As such, I often gravitate toward a less breathable but more waterproof hardshell. Enter Norrona’s More outerwear—a featherweight option that skintrack regulars will appreciate.

The Gore-Tex weatherproofing is on point when winter storm warnings roll in, while the 40D face fabric and a no-frills feature set keep weight low. It’s not the most durable jacket in my closet, but it’s one of the most capable in its weight class and one I reach for on backyard adventures and international trips alike. The low-slung bib is a highlight of the kit, too: the cut is high enough to keep powder from soaking your base layers, but low enough that you won’t overheat as much while touring.

Gram-counting backpackers won’t even consider this Therm-a-Rest NeoLoft Sleeping Pad. But if you prioritize comfort over pack weight, the 4.6-inch-thick NeoLoft is worth considering, especially if you split time between moderate backpacking missions and car camping adventures. My favorite feature is the curvature of the side rails, which keep you from falling off the comfy, supportive, well-insulated sleeping pad onto cold, hard ground.

The tried-and-true Wawona tent series has long been a favorite of Outside staff, but the new Universal 3-person version is an entirely fresh addition to the lineup. Essentially, it’s designed using “universal design” principles, which seek to improve outdoor accessibility and inclusivity. Noteworthy additions on that front include a massive, floor-to-ceiling vestibule that can fit two wheelchairs and a leash attachment for service dogs. This tent is truly universal, though, as any campers will appreciate the vertical space, durable materials, and painless setup.

Equal parts fire pit, grill, and campground centerpiece, Snow Peak’s Takibi will change the way you camp more than any other piece of gear in this guide. Forged from stainless steel, the Japanese-made fire pit is built to last. While the heirloom-quality build isn’t lightweight, intentional design makes folding, transporting, and storing it relatively chill. The raised, removable grill is a welcome tool if you’re looking to get into open-fire cooking—one best paired with wagyu steaks, fresh-caught trout, sizzling prawns, and other such delicacies. And Snow Peak makes veritable mountains of accessories, so you can customize your Takibi setup to your camp cooking needs.

I love DB’s Snow Roller Pro for a few reasons. First off, an internal “ribcage” protects contents in transit. The versatile roller handles both skis and boards thanks to an adjustable length that fits 203-centimeter skis and chubby, volume-shifted snowboards. The wheels are beefy, and the handle connects to the DB 90L Hugger Roller Bag for near-effortless one-handed steering. But as someone with limited gear storage at home, my favorite feature is that the Snow Roller Pro rolls up into a compact, easy-to-stash bundle when you’re not using it. All told, it’s a welcome addition to any powder-chaser’s arsenal, and a great gift for anyone you tolerate enough to invite on a ski trip.

This rolling Black Hole Wheeled Duffel features the same tester-beloved armored fabric, pocket configuration, and heavy-duty lash points. However, it’s much more expensive, but you get a ton of utility for the extra outlay, with oversized wheels and a telescoping handle for easy maneuvering in international airports and crowded subway stations. This is my go-to for powder pilgrimages to Japan, British Columbia, you name it.

The Numbers Game: Man Utd look to build on Champions League against in-form Villa

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

All eyes are once again on Manchester United as they look to turn around their Premier League form against Aston Villa before the international break.

United were winless in nine games ahead of Tuesday's meeting with Roma in the Champions League, and things looked to be getting worse when they found themselves 2-0 down after 17 minutes.

However, they orchestrated a superb second-half comeback to win 3-2 and earn their first victory since September 10, thanks to Matheus Cunha's late brace, and ease some of the pressure on Michael Carrick's shoulders.

But with the threat of dropping into the relegation zone now a very real worry, the test is to translate that form into the top flight against a Villa side on the way up the rankings.

After failing to win any of their first four league games, they are now unbeaten in five (W3 D2), though they come into this match on the back of a 3-0 thrashing at the hands of Barcelona in the Champions League.

This will be Unai Emery's 200th Premier League game as a manager, though, and Villa will want to make sure he celebrates the landmark on a high.

So, who will come out on top at Old Trafford on Sunday? Here, using Opta data, we look at the key storylines.

— Manchester United (@ManUtd) November 5, 2026

There is not much between these teams according to the Opta supercomputer, though United are narrow favourites to clinch all three points, winning 39% of the 10,000 pre-match simulations.

That is helped by the fact that United have only lost two of their 31 Premier League home games against Villa (W24 D5), 1-0 defeats in both 2009-10 and 2021-22.

Villa won their first Premier League match against the Red Devils under Emery in November 2022, but they have won just one of their seven since then (D1 L5).

That victory came in December last year at Villa Park, thanks to Morgan Rogers' brace, but they then lost the reverse fixture 3-1 in March.

Emery's side are still given a 34.5% chance of triumphing this weekend, as they look for a third straight win on the road in the top flight.

There is still a 26.5% chance of a draw, which means a stalemate is fairly likely, but it does not feel like a point will be enough for United this weekend.

The first 17 minutes against Roma seemed to sum up all of United's recent struggles, and they were lucky not to be further behind when the half-time whistle blew.

But they were a vastly different team after the break, helped by Roma's complacency, as Cunha led the charge, winning the penalty from which Bruno Fernandes scored at the second attempt, before bagging himself a brace.

He then got himself sent off with two quickfire bookings – a needless late tackle after excessive celebrations – but his hard work had already got the job done.

4 – No team have won more UEFA Champions League games from two or more goals behind than Manchester United (also Real Madrid). Salvation. https://t.co/sclavosZVa

United's confidence will be buoyed by the dramatics of Tuesday's victory, and Carrick will be looking to replicate the second-half dominance. 10 of their 15 shots against Roma came after the break, with 2.5 of their 2.7 total expected goals (xG) generated coming after the interval.

However, their league form is a very real worry as United are winless in their last seven Premier League games (D4 L3).

Only between April and May 2025 have they had a longer run without winning in the competition (eight games), which was ended by a 2-0 home win over Villa.

Indeed, the Red Devils have just one win in nine Premier League games this season (D4 L4). They have not started a league season with one win in 10 games since 1972-73 (W1 D4 L5).

Only three teams in top-flight history have followed a season where they finished in the top three with as few as one win in their first 10 games – Derby County in 1894-95, Chelsea in 1920-21, and Arsenal in 1953-54.

There are issues at both ends of the pitch for Carrick to address, but their defensive frailty has proven to be their biggest weakness.

United are one of only two sides without a Premier League clean sheet this season, along with Ipswich Town. It is the longest into a league season they have gone without a clean sheet since 1986-87 (10 games).

The last side to finish in the top three of the top flight, then fail to keep a clean sheet in their first 10 games of the next season, were Liverpool in 1966-67.

United have conceded 15 goals in total in the league, with five of those coming across their last two losses against Bournemouth (3-0) and Chelsea (2-0), respectively.

There were signs of positivity in the first half against Chelsea as well, before a second-half capitulation, and consistency across 90 minutes is another concern that needs to be fixed if United want their first league win since MD2 of the competition.

While there were jubilant celebrations at Old Trafford on Tuesday, Villa endured a much different ending against LaLiga champions Barcelona.

It was their first defeat in Europe this season and saw them suffer their joint-heaviest defeat in major European competition, having also lost by three goals to Royal Antwerp in September 1975 (1-4) and Inter in November 1990 (0-3), both in the UEFA Cup.

Moreover, no Premier League side has committed more errors leading to goals in all competitions this season than Villa (seven, level with Sunderland).

But while Emery was frustrated that his side were not more clinical against Barcelona, he will not be dwelling on the result for too long as they aim to continue their rise up the Premier League.

Their five-game unbeaten streak has lifted them up to 10th in the table, and Emery, who is reportedly in advanced talks over a new Villa deal, will be keen to continue their upward trajectory in his landmark match.

He is the fifth Spaniard to reach 200 matches in the Premier League after Rafael Benitez, Roberto Martinez, Pep Guardiola, and Mikel Arteta, with Benitez's (2-0 win) and Arteta's (1-1 draw) 200th games also coming against United.

Unai Emery's first game in charge of the Villa, #OnThisDay four years ago  https://t.co/hpiYbgtARQ

— Aston Villa (@AstonVilla) November 6, 2026

There are good signs that they can come away with all three points, as well, given Villa have won their last two away league matches, as many as in their previous 12 combined (D5 L5). They last had a longer run between November and December 2025 (four).

And a fast start could be the key to letting the nerves creep back in at Old Trafford, given United's recent record.

Villa have scored in the first half of each of their last five Premier League games, having gone five games in a row beforehand without a goal in the first 45 minutes.

The Villans last scored in the first half of six league games in a row between May and August 2023.

In 2026, Fernandes ranks first for each of chances created (104), open-play chances created (80), big chances created (27), successful final-third passes (531), passes/crosses played into the opposition box (289), and throughballs (29).

He is averaging 3.8 chances created per 90 minutes under Carrick, the best by any United player under a single manager in the league since 2003-04 (min. 1,000 mins played).

Since his United debut in February 2020, Fernandes has both taken (50) and scored (44) the most penalties of any Premier League player across all competitions (excluding shoot-outs), after his spot-kick against Roma.

Nicolas Jackson has scored five goals in eight Premier League appearances for Villa.

Crypto ETFs see over half a billion dollars in outflows amid post-midterm market slide

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

U.S. spot crypto ETFs saw more than $500 million in combined net outflows on Thursday as crypto and tech markets fell across the board.

U.S. spot crypto exchange-traded funds witnessed more than half a billion dollars in net outflows on Thursday as markets reversed initial gains following the U.S. midterms election results.

Among bitcoin ETFs, Fidelity's FBTC led Thursday's net outflows with $199.9 million, followed by BlackRock's IBIT with $105 million and Grayscale's GBTC with $49.9 million, according to data compiled by The Block. Franklin Templeton's EZBC, VanEck's HODL, and Ark Invest's ARKB also saw net outflows, while the remaining ETFs posted zero flows for the day.

Ethereum funds also witnessed heavy net outflows, led by BlackRock's ETHA and ETHB, with $75.2 billion and $22 billion in redemptions, respectively. Fidelity's FETH and Grayscale's ETHE saw outflows too.

Other major crypto ETFs didn't fare much better, accounting for size. U.S. Solana ETF saw a combined $1.2 million in net outflows for the day, Zcash ETFs $11 million, and Hyperliquid ETFs $3 million, according to SoSoValue data. XRP ETFs were the only outlier, with zero flows for the day.

Major U.S. crypto ETFs have now seen a combined net outflow of more than $1.1 billion so far this week.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell 5.6% on Thursday, more than erasing an initial 4% gain on Wednesday following Tuesday's U.S. midterm elections. Ether Ethereum (ETH) was hit harder, falling 7.8% for the day, with the GMCI 30 index, representing a selection of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, dropping 6.4%.

Tech stocks also suffered, with the Nasdaq Composite falling 5.5%, and the S&P 500 dropping 4.4% as investors continue to weigh the impact of potential restrictions on the AI infrastructure buildout following the Democrats' sweep of both the House and Senate.

Kyle Rodda, senior financial market analyst at Capital.com, said the Democratic sweep added to concerns over the AI investment boom, with investors already questioning technology valuations and the pace of infrastructure spending.

"The regulatory and political risks arise at a time when the proverbial AI trade was already on shakier foundations," Rodda said, pointing to disappointing earnings and signs of slowing AI spending.

Axel Rudolph, chief technical analyst at IG, told The Block that bitcoin has been "dragged into the AI rout as investors rapidly unwind risk across technology and crypto markets."

Rudolph said bitcoin's correlation with technology stocks had left it exposed to the selloff, with more than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions liquidated in 24 hours as the market continued to decline.

Friday's U.S. jobs report could complicate matters further for markets, particularly if the figures give the Federal Reserve more reason to raise rates.

Economists expect around 60,000 to 65,000 new jobs, according to Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com. She warned that a stronger jobs report, particularly one showing higher wage growth, could send both Treasury yields and the dollar higher.

Despite the broader market weakness, André Dragosch, head of research for Europe at Bitwise, expects bitcoin to outperform U.S. equities as the AI trade unwinds.

Dragosch said bitcoin is now more sensitive to the dollar and expectations for monetary policy than to economic growth, and could decouple from stocks if financial conditions improve.

In Charting the Unknown, Crimson Desert doubles down on its maximalism in audacious, faintly sinister style

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

And so we are back in Pywel: the super-sized and often super-pretty realm where the deserts are blood-red, the grass is a lurid shade of green, and Kliff and his merry gang of Greymanes seem to be pissed all the time. Yet what's this? Peace seems to have broken out across the region, which means our grizzled action hero is in a reflective mood.

At the outset of Charting the Unknown, Crimson Desert's recently released DLC, I'm tasked with taking Kliff out for a quiet spot of moonlit fishing. The problem is, I seem to have forgotten how to actually fish - and if memory serves, perhaps I never learned because the system was so poorly explained in the base game. So I flail around for a full 15 minutes before eventually relenting and consulting Reddit on how to snag a bite.

You might reasonably assume that these types of basic explanatory wrinkles had been ironed out in the countless updates since Crimson Desert's release in March. Certainly, developer Pearl Abyss has gone to great effort elsewhere to streamline what was, at times, a frustratingly clunky experience (those menus at launch, oh god) while adding even more stuff, such as all-new cutscenes (to improve the story's legibility) and the ability to ride wolves and tigers (because why the hell not). But for all the tinkering and wholesale additions, Crimson Desert has retained its essential character: the menus remain a total faff and the narrative, which I started afresh some weeks ago, is still bafflingly incoherent (maybe even more than I initially appreciated).

It's into this churn of ongoing development that Pearl Abyss has produced Charting the Unknown, a DLC which adds even more elements to a game already buckling under the weight of mechanical overload. Now you can build ships and sail them around Pywel's azure-blue waters. You can dive below the waves and swim with the fishes. You can also fall in love, get married, and - should things not pan out - get divorced. The everything game has become, well, even more everything in quintessentially audacious style. On that note, just wait until you see some of its supermassive Evangelion-esque maritime bosses.

Though don't get too excited. Like the base game, the DLC is a mixed bag. First, shipfaring lacks any kind of drama. Islands are so spread out as to make the actual journey painfully boring: impressionistic oceanic voyaging à la Wind Waker this is not. The diving, though, is beautifully executed. Underwater locales are notably serene, free from many of the irritating distractions that plague ground traversal.

As for the new narrative threads involving all three playable characters - grizzled Kliff, Bayonetta-alike Damiane, and gentle giant orc Oongka - they're equally uneven. Kliff's is the most interesting, transplanting the player to a desert cloaked in a thick orange sandstorm that, without the aid of a working compass, must be mapped out using beacons resembling luminescent dongs. It's pleasingly disorientating, a rare example of Pearl Abyss pushing a kind of intentional design friction, rather than accidentally creating it by inadequately explaining how to interact with the game.

Alas, there are also bugs, one of which was game-halting: Damiane's steed wouldn't spawn when I needed to follow Kliff on horseback. It was a reminder that for all the visual splendour, Crimson Desert can often feel like it's being held together by the code equivalent of gaffer tape.

Everything controls about as well as you'd expect if you've played the base game, i.e. not very well at all. Kliff may be able to run on water and soar through the sky like a cross between Matrix star Neo and Jesus, but he remains a bastard to control for even the most basic tasks. Why can't I lock onto the object a foot away? Why is he doing a three-hit combo somersault through the air when all I tried to do is pull out his lantern? These issues are compounded by the new romance and socialising options that appear every time you target an NPC: Crimson Desert becomes even fiddlier as it introduces these additional layers.

As the hours pass by - as I run, gallop, and bound through the arresting landscape in a manner that is comparatively frictionless compared to the rest of the game - my mind begins to numb. For all the extraneous mechanical gubbins, missions remain brainlessly simple: cavort from one waypoint to another and do a few simple button presses, or crack a few skulls to chug the narrative along. But then perhaps a certain numbing is the point of Crimson Desert? At a time when the real world is markedly inhospitable (diminishing job prospects, the ongoing cost-of-living crisis, the looming threat of geopolitical meltdown), Pearl Abyss' epic swaddles the player like a swords-and-sorcery comfort blanket. It is a safe space to disappear into for hours. Leave your worldly woes at the start screen.

The impression this mash-up of a game appears to want to impart is generosity: here, Pearl Abyss seems to say, is a product capable of accommodating your every wish, will, and whim. It appears to have left no gameplay stone unturned in its ultimate quest to provide the player with total satisfaction - a kind of blissed-out contentment at the thought of never having to leave this almost divine realm.

But rather than generous, perhaps there is something nefarious about Crimson Desert's maximalist design philosophy. It strikes me again that this is the most uniquely modern title I've ever played, as if the Grand Theft Auto and Assassin's Creed franchises, countless MMORPGs (the genre in which Pearl Abyss cut its teeth), and many hyper-compulsive mobile titles were squished together in an attention-devouring final form. One so mightily captivating, so singularly consuming, that the player keeps playing until their lifeforce is depleted and they can play no more.

That seems to be the way these "forever" games curve: toward the player's surrender - of mind, body, and time. And at least until Grand Theft Auto 6 is released in a few weeks, Crimson Desert and its new DLC seem to be the purest expression yet of this type of thing. Wading through its sheer mass of hit-or-miss distractions (which could expand even more), the design ethos begins to grate. As if I'm stuck in an elaborate, endlessly bifurcating maze of hamster wheels, always busy, though never enjoying any discernible pay-off. And so, as its presentational lustre wears off further, and its eye-popping massiveness becomes less of a novelty, Crimson Desert begins to hit differently: much less high-fantasy heaven and more like hell.

He joins James Garner, Elliot Anderson, Declan Rice and Myles Lewis-Skelly as Tuchel’s central midfield options with Manchester United star Mainoo among the list of high profile absentees which once again includes Cole Palmer and Phil Foden.

Thomas Tuchel doubles down on Cole Palmer warning as England snub explained

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Chelsea, featuring team news and expert analysis from Dom Smith

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Thomas Tuchel has explained why Cole Palmer was left out of the latest England squad, repeating his belief that the Chelsea star “misses too many opportunities to impress” for his country.

Palmer was on Friday the headline omission from Tuchel’s 25-man squad to contest this month’s Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain.

That was despite England staff checking with Chelsea on his fitness this week in the lead-up to the squad being finalised. Chelsea insist that Palmer is fit and was available for selection.

Having missed the World Cup in the summer, Palmer was initially picked for the last England squad in September, but withdrew due to an injury which Chelsea said they needed to manage.

While Tuchel acknowledged at the time that the issue was genuine, he did not hide his disappointment and remarked that Palmer “misses too many opportunities” to play for England.

Of Tuchel’s 26 matches in charge so far, Palmer has featured in only three. The England head coach was asked at a press conference at Wembley on Friday why Palmer had been overlooked.

“It’s enough No10s and we stick to the guys who were in camp [last time],” Tuchel explained. “The impact of all the players was immense.

“We have Josh King as a new player. He can play all four [attacking] positions. That’s basically the reason. He gives us a bit of flexibility.

“In the pre-season, he [King] helped us in training as a full-back. We will not try that again.”

Tuchel provided further detail about Palmer’s situation, insisting: “Everyone is desperate to play for England, and Cole is no exception.

“But, again, what I said last time: he misses too many opportunities to impress. Two-and-a-half weeks was a long time [to miss]. The door is always open, but nothing has changed, and we decided [to go] without him.”

There was a first senior England call-up for Borussia Dortmund midfielder Jobe Bellingham, the younger brother of Real Madrid star Jude.

Tuchel hinted that Jobe Bellingham could come into his thinking as he plots out his midfield selection for the clash with Croatia next week.

NYSE-backed crypto venture faces 90% stock rejection as $92 liquidity problem threatens tokenization dream

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Apple, Nvidia and 65 other companies have objected to OKXICE's plans, while thin liquidity across existing tokenized stock markets raises a second major obstacle to its launch.

More than 90% of the stocks targeted by OKX and NYSE-backed tokenization venture now face objections from their issuers.

A comparison of OKXICE's latest regulatory notice with its original October proposal shows that companies have objected to 57 of the 63 stock tickers initially earmarked for trading.

The objections, which have grown to 67 companies overall, include Apple, Nvidia, Microsoft, Amazon, Tesla, and Meta, threatening to strip the proposed blockchain-based marketplace of many of Wall Street's most widely traded names before its launch.

The setback comes just weeks after the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) introduced a regulatory exemption designed to accelerate tokenized stock trading, allowing qualifying platforms to offer blockchain-based shares through automated trading pools.

OKXICE, a joint venture equally owned by crypto exchange OKX's US holding company and Intercontinental Exchange, the parent of the New York Stock Exchange, plans to let investors trade tokenized versions of US stocks around the clock using stablecoins.

However, the growing corporate resistance has left the venture facing questions about which securities it can offer and whether investors will have sufficient liquidity to trade them.

The scale of the resistance has grown rapidly since OKXICE outlined its plans in early October.

By Oct. 30, 50 companies had registered objections. That figure rose to 62 by Nov. 4 before another five companies, including Meta, Palantir, S&P Global, KLA and Super Micro Computer, joined the list on Nov. 5.

Of the original 62 companies identified by OKXICE, 56 have now objected. The original proposal covered 63 tickers because Alphabet's two share classes were listed separately.

That leaves just six companies from the initial lineup without disclosed objections: Strategy, Coinbase, Robinhood, Circle, Securitize and BitGo.

All six have substantial connections to the cryptocurrency or digital asset industry, highlighting how far the venture's potential stock selection has shifted from the broader US equity market.

However, the absence of an objection does not establish that those companies have endorsed the proposal or that their tokenized shares will be available when trading begins.

The resistance is particularly significant because of how the SEC structured its September exemption.

Under the framework, companies whose shares are tokenized by unaffiliated third parties must receive written notice and an opportunity to object. A formal objection prevents the venue from offering those third-party tokenized shares under the exemption.

The framework leaves room for issuer-backed tokenization, meaning the objections do not necessarily represent opposition to blockchain-based securities in general.

Nevertheless, OKXICE has yet to announce a launch date or confirm how many stocks will ultimately be available. Its public website continues to advertise the trading venue as forthcoming.

Start with the stories that matter. Get the context behind the headlines and a clear view of what to watch next.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Even if OKXICE secures a viable selection of stocks, it faces another challenge: attracting enough capital to support trading.

The tokenization venture disclosed that neither it nor its affiliates will provide liquidity to its proposed marketplace.

Instead, eligible outside participants, including market makers and institutional investors, would need to deposit stock tokens and stablecoins into trading pools that facilitate transactions.

That model places considerable importance on the willingness of independent investors to commit capital, particularly outside traditional US market hours.

Existing tokenized equity markets offer a warning about the difficulties involved.

According to Dune's After Issuance report, the median decentralized trading pool for tokenized equities held just $92 as of Aug. 31.

Across 5,484 pools containing $69 million in assets, just 78 pools accounted for approximately $56 million, or 81% of the measured holdings. By protocol, Dune located $34 million on Uniswap, $17 million on PancakeSwap, and $11 million on Raydium.

This shows how concentrated liquidity remains across decentralized equity markets, despite growing interest in bringing traditional securities on-chain.

The $92 figure represents the median holdings of existing pools, rather than the liquidity available for every stock or a forecast of trading conditions on OKXICE.

The venture plans to use Uniswap v4-based pools on OKX's XLayer blockchain, where tokenized shares will trade against stablecoins through approved wallets.

Unlike conventional stock exchanges that match buyers and sellers through order books, the proposed system relies on assets deposited into pools to execute trades.

Insufficient capital in those pools could cause significant price slippage, particularly when investors try to execute large transactions or sell positions during market volatility.

That creates a significant challenge for a venture promising access to US equities beyond Wall Street's regular trading hours.

OKXICE must now overcome substantial issuer resistance while convincing outside market participants to finance trading in whatever tokenized stocks remain available.

Its eventual launch will provide the first meaningful test of whether the venue can assemble enough eligible securities and trading capital to deliver on its round-the-clock ambitions.
